## Ten-task pass — 2026-08-18 (night) — `implemenation/previous/PLAN_ten-tasks-2026-08-18-night.md`

**Instruction:** *"si des taches sont completes, choisir des nouvelles 10 taches ouverts, creer d'un
plan d'implementation et continuer avec l'execution, chaque etape, veuillez mettre a jour [this
prompt] pour des sessions demaines."* This section is updated as each task lands, so tomorrow's
session inherits the state without reading the whole plan.

🔵 **Selection rule applied for the first time** (the one the previous pass had to learn): every
candidate was checked against `openings/extra/` and `openubem/outputs/comparisons/` **before**
selection. **None of these ten was already answered.**

| task | status |
|---|---|
| W01–W04 | ✅ **OPEN-56 PROVED BY INTERVENTION** — six of six failures fixed by one field; control 16/16 vs 0/16; cost bounded at **+0.75 % mean** and one-directional |
| W05 | ✅ **the writer is localised** |
| W06 | ✅ **first fleet-wide error taxonomy this project has ever had** — 8,160 files, 123 families |
| W07 | ✅ **OPEN-09 sharpened, and the result is not what the item implies** |
| W08 | ✅ **OPEN-35's 2,611 / 32.00 % reproduces exactly** |
| W09 | ✅ **OPEN-12/14's 2,806 / 34.39 % reproduces exactly** |
| W10 | records |

### 🔵 W01–W04 — the OPEN-56 experiment, and it settles OPEN-42 too

**Design.** `Zone.Volume = floor_area × height` written into a **copy** of each IDF; both arms re-run
from the same file in the same session (**the days-old `sim_out` results were deliberately not used as
the baseline**). Sample fixed before any result was seen: OPEN-42's six failures + ten successes.
Diff asserted object-by-object first — **`OK n zones, field 9 only`** on all sixteen.

✅ **Control:** `Indicated Zone Volume <= 0.0` in **16 / 16** baseline runs, **0 / 16** treated.

🔴 **Mechanism, six of six:** every OPEN-42 failure **completes with zero severe errors** after the one
field is written — 11→0, 25→0, 25→0, 9→0, 21→0, 39→0. **An intervention with a control, not an
association off a census. OPEN-42's question is answered: nothing is wrong with those six buildings.**

🔵 **Cost:** ten successes, two cells — **mean +0.75 %, median +0.67 %, sd 0.59, range −0.07 % to
+1.67 %.** **Nine of ten move the same way**, so the stub **understates** energy; the absolute Δ is
uniform (+0.51 to +1.30 kWh/m²) across buildings spanning 41–524, i.e. a fixed per-zone effect.
⚠️ **n = 10, two rural cells — a bound and a sign, not a fleet estimate. The +0.75 % → ≈+1.2 kWh/m²
arithmetic is deliberately NOT performed as a correction, and `157.1` needs no restating.**

⚠️ **The experiment failed its own control on the first attempt** — 0/16 in *both* arms, because these
IDFs use `HVACTemplate:*` and EnergyPlus refuses them without `ExpandObjects` (`-x`). Every run died
at input processing. **The control caught it, which is what a control is for.** Anyone re-running any
IDF from this pipeline by hand must pass `-x`.

### W05 — where the reversed winding comes from

**It is not our code.** No OpenUBEM module sets floor or ceiling vertex order; the order is produced
by **`geomeppy/geom/polygons.py:573-611`** (`normalize_coords` → `set_entry_direction`), which orients
each polygon against an "outside point" derived from the `GlobalGeometryRules` entry direction. Our
IDFs declare `UpperLeftCorner / Counterclockwise / Relative`.

🔴 **OpenUBEM already has a detector for exactly this signal and deliberately does not use it.**
`openubem/idf/surfaces.py:223` (`_coreperim_has_inverted_winding`) computes the negative-signed-area
test; its caller at `:671-681` excludes it with the stated reason *"EnergyPlus convention always uses
negative signed-area (CW winding) for floor surfaces; checking sign would produce false positives on
healthy buildings."* **That reasoning is why nobody looked** — and EnergyPlus itself now reports these
floors as upside-down on 8,160 of 8,160 buildings and derives a negative volume from them. **Stated as
a tension to resolve, not as a verdict: 2-D signed area is projection-dependent, and the observable
fact is the negative volume, not the sign.**

### W06 — the fleet error taxonomy (new artifact)

`openubem/outputs/comparisons/open09_fleet_err_taxonomy.csv` (123 families) and
`open09_fleet_err_perbuilding.csv` (8,160 rows). Every prior fleet error census ran against the E02
harvest, which the 2026-08-17 sweep emptied; **this one runs on a corpus that is still on disk and
whose inputs are frozen.**

**Nine families are universal — present in all 8,160 buildings.** Two of them are OPEN-56's:
`GetVertices: Floor is upside down!` (44,455 occurrences) and `GetVertices: Roof/Ceiling is upside
down!` (44,433). ✅ **The `Indicated Zone Volume <= 0.0` families sum to exactly 8,160 buildings**
(7,673 `_WHOLE` + 385 `_PERIM` + 98 + 2 + 2), which independently re-derives OPEN-56's 100 % by a
different method — the first census counted a log literal, this one partitions by zone-name pattern.

⚠️ **One alarming-looking family checked and cleared.** `Output:Meter: invalid Key Name` fires
**52,932 times across all 8,160 buildings**. Control over 1,935 buildings in four cells:
**every building with elevator energy has its elevator meter, and every building carrying the
`ELEVATORS:...` invalid-meter warning has zero elevator energy — 1,358 / 577, no overlap at all.**
The warnings are EnergyPlus correctly reporting meters with no contributing objects. **Nothing here,
and it corroborates OPEN-46's closure from a direction that item never used.**

### W07 — OPEN-09, and the finding is a strong one

**Heat-balance non-convergence is rare, not fleet-wide: 16 of 8,160 buildings = 0.20 %**, all of them
in LA cells, every one with **exactly 15 warnings** (standard deviation 0 — a capped report).

🔴 **But the contingency is perfect.** All six fleet failures are inside those 16; **no building
without a non-convergence warning fails.**

| | succeeded | failed |
|---|---:|---:|
| no non-convergence warning | **8,144** | **0** |
| ≥1 non-convergence warning | 10 | **6** |

**Read it carefully: non-convergence is a *necessary* condition for failure in this fleet and not a
sufficient one** — 10 of the 16 survive. Together with OPEN-56 that gives the two-stage picture the
arc has been missing: **the 10 m³ stub is universal (necessary, nowhere near sufficient); the
non-convergence is rare and contains every failure.** OPEN-09's *"cosmetic"* verdict survives as a
statement about prevalence and **fails as a statement about consequence.**

### W08 / W09 — two of the register's oldest figures re-derived, both exact

| figure | register (2026-08-06) | re-derived on run 2 | |
|---|---|---|---|
| OPEN-35 — neither `levels` nor `height_m` | 2,611 / 8,160 = 32.00 % | **2,611 / 8,160 = 32.00 %** | ✅ reproduces |
| OPEN-12 / OPEN-14 — `height_m` null | 2,806 / 8,160 = 34.39 % | **2,806 / 8,160 = 34.39 %** | ✅ reproduces |

**And OPEN-35's mechanism holds at 100 %:** all 2,611 reach the results file persisted at
`levels = 1.0`. OPEN-12's three 100 % cells (`nyc_suburban`, `nyc_rural`, `austin_rural`) reproduce
exactly. **Both items may now cite a corpus that still exists and can be re-run**, which the E02-based
originals cannot. Evidence: `openubem/outputs/comparisons/open35_open12_input_recensus.csv`.

### The three results that change what the next session should believe

**1. OPEN-56 is measured at fleet scale and its cost model is NOT what we thought.**
Control whole: `Indicated Zone Volume <= 0.0` in **70 / 70** baseline runs, **0 / 70** treated,
**70 / 70 completed in both arms**, diff assertion 70/70. Cost **+0.98 % mean / +0.84 % median**,
range −0.23 % to +3.25 %, **65 / 69 positive**, absolute Δ mean **+1.00 kWh/m²**.
🔴 **The "fixed per-zone effect" from the night pass is refuted** — `corr(pct_change, n_zones)
= +0.113`, and the absolute Δ is the *more* stable normalisation (cv **0.79** raw vs **1.09** per
zone). It is a **fixed per-building offset**. Cell ordering is **geographic** (LA high ~1.8, NYC low
~0.4, Austin ~1.0), which sits next to OPEN-19. ⚠️ **`157.1` was deliberately NOT restated** — 5 per
cell is stratified, not population-weighted. **The remedy is still unauthorised and still needs a
ruling: write `Zone.Volume`, or fix the winding.**

**2. OPEN-09 and OPEN-56 are INDEPENDENT — do not fold them.** The ten non-convergent-but-successful
buildings show **150 non-convergence warnings baseline, 150 treated, 15/15 unchanged on every one**,
while the same treatment clears the volume warning completely. Both facts stand together: the
treatment repairs all six failures *and* leaves the non-convergence untouched in the ten that survive.
**A session that merges these two items would be undoing a measured result.**

**3. Two blockers were stale, and both for the same reason — nobody had looked at the disk.**
OPEN-08 was blocked on *"no prior-generation source carries `vintage_standard`"*: **the E02 parquet
manifests SURVIVED the 2026-08-17 sweep** (61 files, all twelve cells). 🔴 **"E02 is gone" is too
strong and must stop being repeated** — what is gone is E02's `.sql` and `.idf`. OPEN-14's named next
step was taken: **zero `FUSED` provenance tokens across all 8,160 buildings**, including `nyc_centre`,
the one cell that *has* a tracked Overture slice — so the missing slices are real but **not the
binding blocker**, and the OPEN-12 convergence is a coincidence of coverage.

### Two mistakes this pass made, both caught by its own controls

1. **The OPEN-08 comparison dropped the geometry column**, which silently disables
   `resolve_vintage`'s tier-1 spatial donor (`knn_fill` degrades to the group mode without raising).
   It produced **0.3554 % — ten times too large.** The tell was **no `HOTDECK_*` rows in the tier
   table**. 🔵 **Lesson for any re-derivation of an imputation output: print the tier distribution and
   check it against the fleet's own `data_quality_flag` census before quoting a number.**
2. **140 EnergyPlus jobs through a 6-worker pool left ten output directories completely empty** — no
   `.err`, no severe, nothing. That reads as failure and would have reported the control as 60/70.
   Serially, the identical file completes in **18 seconds with 0 severe**. 🔵 **An empty output
   directory is not a failed simulation. Cap EnergyPlus parallelism low, or verify empties serially
   before scoring them.**

### For the next session — the shortest useful summary

1. 🔴 **Still owed by the user, now more than a day old: the OPEN-55 ruling** —
   `extra/PROPOSAL_open-55_unknown-pde-bounds.md` §10, Option B vs B+. **Nothing is patched until it
   comes.** This is the single oldest blocker on the board.
2. 🔴 **Four closures now stand recommended and untaken, all the user's call:** **OPEN-42** and
   **OPEN-11** (fold into OPEN-56), **OPEN-07**, and **OPEN-08** (new this pass). Do not take them
   unilaterally; do re-surface them.
3. 🔵 **OPEN-56's remedy needs a ruling, not more measurement.** Its cost is now bounded on all twelve
   cells with a whole control. What is unresolved is a code tension, stated and not decided:
   `openubem/idf/surfaces.py:223` has a detector for exactly this signal, disabled at `:671-681` on
   the stated ground that the sign test would give false positives — while EnergyPlus reports these
   floors as upside-down on 8,160 of 8,160 buildings.
4. 🔴 **A NEW lead worth one task, opened by X01 and deliberately not generalised:** on **1 of 60**
   buildings (`nyc_centre/relation_3566904`) writing `Zone.Volume` also changed the reported **Total
   Building Area, 157,115 → 37,551 m² (÷4.18)**. 59 of 60 were identical to within 0.1 %. **The
   project's EUI denominator is EnergyPlus's own simulated floor area**, so if this is not unique it
   reaches the denominator, not just the numerator. **The next step is a cheap census: compare
   `.eio` simulated floor area against `footprint_area × levels` fleet-wide.**
5. 🔵 **OPEN-35's next step is named and not done:** its consequence cannot be settled
   cross-sectionally (the +47.9 % is composition). **It needs an intervention with a control** —
   rebuild a sample at a corrected storey count, run both arms — exactly what worked for OPEN-56.
6. 🔴 **`%LOCALAPPDATA%/Temp/ubem_validation/open48_refleet/` is load-bearing for at least seven live
   items and is NOT durable.** 152 GB sits under that tree, 95 % of it `.sql` — the same class the
   2026-08-17 sweep deleted elsewhere. **Check it exists before planning anything on it.** The
   material actually cited is **under 0.12 GB** (`.err` + vector/CSV), so preserving the evidence is
   cheap if the user ever authorises it. **Nothing was moved this pass.**
7. **Any hand-run of a pipeline IDF needs `energyplus -x`** (`HVACTemplate:*`). Unchanged.
8. **Before selecting items, grep `openings/extra/` and `openubem/outputs/comparisons/`.** The §1
   table is not an index of what has been measured — and as of this pass, *everything* has been

## 2026-08-19 (later) — `PLAN_open-57-and-58_2026-08-19.md` (T01–T08) + `PLAN_board-17-ready-2026-08-19.md` (T01–T06)

**Register: 23 live / 35 struck / 58 total, next free `OPEN-59` — unchanged. Nothing closed; four
closures recommended and left to the user.**

### OPEN-57 — RESOLVED (T02–T05)

Two faults, not one, and neither was transient.

1. **Deterministic remote-command length limit.** Bisected to single-id resolution: **565 ids parse,
   566 do not.** 🔴 The first *failing* wrapper is **8,191 B — one byte below 8,192 — and fails
   anyway**, so "it's an 8192 buffer" is contradicted by the data. No round number matches at any of
   probe / wrapper / argv. Content proved innocent across three variants at both lengths.
2. **CRLF on stdin — found only because the first fix still read 0.** `subprocess.run(text=True,
   input=...)` rewrites LF to CRLF **on Windows**, so ids reached the remote loop with a trailing
   carriage return and every path test failed silently. 🔴 **This returns a clean, plausible,
   wrong answer and is invisible on Linux.** The 0/226 was very nearly accepted as a purged
   directory; a stdin-only control (`GOT=1589`) and one `ls` refuted that.

Remedy in `_ssh` only — stdin + raw bytes; `stdin_data=None` byte-for-byte unchanged; OPEN-54's
raise preserved. Live: **225/226**, the exact figure already on record. Suite **1,896 / 55**.

### OPEN-58 — BLAST RADIUS MEASURED (T06–T08)

3 real importers, 6 descended artifacts, **1 unsound row**, 0 unverifiable. 🔴 **The item's own
stated mechanism was wrong:** defect (a) is the **shared process cwd** used by EnergyPlus's `-x`
step, not a shared `-d` outdir — so all three importers are exposed and concurrency is not the
discriminator. ✅ **Zero cited figures corrupted** — defect (b) shifts absolute EUI but every cited
number is a relative change on the same building.

### OPEN-35 — SCOPE CORRECTED AND FIX WIRED (board T04–T06)

🔴 **The ceiling of 11 was wrong; it is 21.** The census was apartment-scoped; 10 `LargeHotel`
buildings consume imputed levels by the identical route, and the 11 are a **strict subset** of the
21. Naive 509-building variant rejected. 🔴 **T04 landed the fix inert** (no call site passed the
new arguments) and said so; **T05 wired 2 of 3 and stopped at a quoted obstacle; T06 closed it** —
leaving 2-of-3 would have made the build and parse paths derive different floor counts for the same
21, recreating the defect elsewhere. All three paths now change the identical 21 osm_ids, verified
by set membership fleet-wide, with a test pinning the three-way agreement.

### OPEN-47 — REMAINING QUESTION ANSWERED (board T03)

**598 buildings diverge, every one a promotion, zero demotions**, so the dropped floor-count test is
not neutral. **167 (27.9 %) have no floor count at all** → sequence any adoption **after** OPEN-35.
Overlap with OPEN-35's changed set: **0**. ⚠️ §1 row reconciled against the §-section, which had
already recorded the citation question adjudicated.

### Director notes

- **Executors stall when they background a command and wait for a notification.** One paused twice
  on a pytest run that could never wake it. Every dispatch now says: foreground only, never wait.
- **Both plans' executors reported honestly against their own interest** — one flagged an
  inconclusive live result rather than claiming a pass, another stopped at a ceiling, a third
  reported its fix as unreachable. All three were right to.
- **Board republished** to the same URL: **"ready" is now 0** (was 17).
- ⚠️ **OPEN-55's acceptance test was NOT run** and is not authorised by the OPEN-57 fix.
- ⚠️ **No fleet figure restated.** The baseline predates the OPEN-35 fix and is stale w.r.t. 21
  buildings; restatement is the user's call.

The item's §-section named **two** acceptable endings — adopt the source's full rule, **or** record

*write up a decision already taken*, **not** *reverse it*. Had this been read as "adopt the full
rule", the pass would have silently overturned a user ruling on a design question while believing it
was obeying one. **T03's own measurement strengthened that ruling's stated reason rather than
undermining it** — 167/598 (27.9 %) carry no observed floor count.

1,589 buildings). It stops at **CP-1** and reports. **Do not dispatch a second executor against the

### 🔴 T03 precondition the plan did not state — the fleet re-run will refuse to start without it

`scripts/validation/open48_fleet_run3.py` carries a `_preflight()` that **refuses to launch unless
every one of the 12 cells already has a seeded `01_buildings.gpkg`** under
`%TEMP%/ubem_validation/<subdir>/`. This is not a nuisance check — **it is what makes the re-run a
single-variable, code-only comparison.** With the cache seeded, `step1_fetch` loads from disk
(`v12_cell_pipeline.py:138-141`) instead of re-fetching OSM, so the geometry is byte-identical to the
run behind the published figure and **every delta is attributable to code**. Re-fetching would silently
mix an input change into a measurement of a code change, and the resulting number would be
uninterpretable rather than merely wrong.

**So T03 is three steps, not one:** copy the frozen inputs into the new `open48_refleet4` subdir
(**copy, never move** — the frozen set is evidence for OPEN-55 too), then run the launcher, then
verify preflight passed before walking away. Pinned parameters recovered from run 3:
`MAX_PARALLEL = 4` — **not run 2's 6**, because six concurrent cells saturated the SSH link to
`speed-submit2` and killed `la_rural` on an scp and `austin_urban` on a `squeue` poll —
`STAGGER_S = 240`, `POLL_S = 30`, and submission in waves under the ≈ 20k task cap.

*"toujours mettre à jour du prompt du directeur … pour des sessions prochaines."* **This document is
the first thing a fresh session with no memory reads.** A task recorded only in a plan doc is
invisible to the next session. Update it every pass, unasked, and when a change reverses an earlier
instruction, say so where a skimming reader cannot miss it.

🟢 **This is the only closure this week that rests on a falsifiable prediction that could
have failed.** The other three closed on judgement or on ratifying an existing user ruling. Weight

⚠️ **The first three attempts each simulated ZERO buildings** and each was a blind retry of
an undiagnosed fault (OPEN-57). The standing lesson: *implemented + ruled ≠ proven*. Check whether

### 🔴 OPEN-59 — opened by auditing the run that PASSED

**Do not skip this because CP-1 reads as a success.** The director recomputed the end-use split
directly from `05_results.csv` rather than accepting the executor's summary, and found:

| median kWh/m² | Unknown (290) | Classified (1,299) | ratio |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| equipment | 37.5 | 43.4 | **0.86×** |
| heating | 140.1 | 99.0 | 1.4× |
| DHW | 103.5 | 42.5 | 2.4× |
| cooling | 25.0 | 4.5 | **5.6×** |
| lighting | 26.7 | 4.0 | **6.7×** |
| **total** | **349.4** | **202.8** | **1.72×** |

🟢 The equipment row **proves the OPEN-55 screen worked** — Unknown is now *below*
classified. 🔴 **But total demand did not follow: the excess relocated rather than
disappearing.** OPEN-55's condition was about *divergences*, which are gone; the *level* of Unknown
demand was never its test. Hence a new item rather than a reopening.

**Two live hypotheses, different remedies, not yet distinguished:** (1) the same widened-bounds
mechanism OPEN-55 found in the equipment column also affects DHW/lighting/occupancy — the B+ screen
only removed donors **on the equipment column** — in which case this is OPEN-55's sibling and the
same column-wise fix applies; or (2) the donor pool is genuinely higher-intensity, which is
calibration, not a defect. **Distinguishing them is cheap, local, and needs no cluster time:** compare
the drawn PDE columns for the 290 Unknown buildings against the donor-table bounds per column, exactly
as OPEN-55 §3 did for equipment alone. **That is the obvious next task and it is not yet dispatched.**

### ⚠️ The generalisable lesson, worth carrying beyond this item

**A dominant defect masks the others in the same aggregate.** Unknown equipment demand was 2,692
W/m²; nothing else in the column was visible next to it. Fixing it did not reveal a clean
baseline — it revealed four more elevated columns. **When a large single-cause defect is fixed,
re-audit the whole aggregate rather than only re-checking the metric that defect broke.** The pass
condition and the health of the system are not the same question.

A cell is done when its directory contains `05_results.csv`. **Twelve such directories = T03 complete.**

### Constraints that still bind this run

- Driver `scripts/validation/open48_fleet_run4.py`, `OUTPUT_SUBDIR = "open48_refleet4"`,
  **`MAX_PARALLEL = 4`** — *not* run 2's 6; six concurrent cells saturated the SSH link to
  `speed-submit2` and killed `la_rural` on an scp and `austin_urban` on a `squeue` poll.
  `STAGGER_S = 240`, `POLL_S = 30`, submit in waves under the Speed ≈ 20k task cap.
- 🔴 **Never run compute on the login node.** `sbatch --array`, fire-and-forget, read the
  output file. Login node = `mkdir`, `scp`, `tar`, `squeue`, `sacct` only.
- The remote shell is **tcsh**; always go through the `_ssh()` helper
  (`scripts/cluster/t08_harvest_results.py:104`), never a bare command string.
- 🔴 **The frozen-input contract is the point of this run.** Inputs were copied from
  `open48_refleet3` and verified against all 12 MD5s before launch, so geometry is byte-identical to
  the run behind 157.1 and **every delta is attributable to code**. If any cell is ever re-fetched
  from OpenStreetMap instead of loaded from cache, that cell's number is **uninterpretable, not
  merely wrong** — discard it and re-seed rather than reporting it.

### What is left after the run lands

1. **T04** — harvest 12 cells; pooled EUI (**total energy ÷ total floor area**, never a mean of
   cell means — OPEN-43); per-cell success **and** failure counts with causes; confirm the 21
   OPEN-35 buildings changed; **plus the four OPEN-59 measurements (a–d)** added to the dispatch
   at CP-1.
2. **T05** — write `../extra/MEASUREMENT_fleet-restatement-2026-08-19.md`.
3. **CP-2, director's own work** — restate the baseline in the **register**, the **checklist**, the
   **board** (`https://claude.ai/code/artifact/0615b50a-75d6-49c6-a354-d4f2f74d3639`, update in
   place, flip row **Y5** from `open`), and **this prompt**. The executor reports the figure;
   🔴 **the executor does not publish it.**
4. **Amend the plan's §4/§7 T03** to include the seeding step — deferred twice now, only
   because the executor holds that file. Do it once the file is released.

### 🔴 Not dispatched, and it should be

The **OPEN-59 local diagnostic**: compare the drawn PDE columns for the 290 Unknown buildings against
the donor-table bounds **per column**, exactly as OPEN-55 §3 did for equipment alone. It decides
between the two hypotheses (sibling defect vs. genuine calibration), it is **local, cheap, and needs
no cluster time**, and it can run *while* the fleet re-run continues — they touch nothing in common.

`implemenation/PLAN_layout-assign-untrimmed-2026-08-19.md`, T01–T04, **CP-1 signed by the director**.
48 buildings (4 per cell × 12, at the 10/35/65/90th percentile positions of `footprint_area_m2`,
run-4 `open48_refleet4` fixtures, integer index, no RNG), rebuilt in `layout_assign` with
**`trim_outputs=False`** as the only variable changed, simulated locally in isolated per-building
`run_dir`s (OPEN-58 guard), parsed with production `parse_building()`.

🟢 **48/48 parsed `success` — 0 build, 0 simulation, 0 parse failures.** The cross-cutting blocker
recorded at CP-3 of the twenty-item plan (T15/T18/T20a all stopped at it) is **retired**:
`trim_outputs=False` is the whole fix, demonstrated across 12 cells, 9 archetypes, 1–18 storeys,
1–90 zones. **No `layout_assign` question in this arc is blocked on measurement capability any more.**

🔵 **First HEAD-consistent, generation-clean cross-mode numbers, SAMPLE ONLY (n=48):** pooled
**−26.25 %** (108.64 vs 147.30), median per building **−24.40 %**. Pooled and median agree in sign
and within 2 points. 47 of 48 below `auto`; sole exception `austin_rural way/1165379866` at +2.67 %.
Corroborates the −29 % this arc was opened on.

🔴 **The OPEN-10 floor-area confounder inflates the gap but does not cause it.** Area-agreeing
subset n=21 pools to −19.73 % / median −25.87 %; that pooled figure is **75 % carried by one
88,309 m² Courthouse**, and excluding it the other 20 pool to **−28.02 %** / median −26.05 %.
**A real ≈−25 % energy-side difference survives. Nothing in this arc explains it — this is the
live question the pass hands you.**

⚪ **OPEN-18 is NOT sized.** Its slice (10/35 slots, four NYC cells) is **n = 8**: −27.98 % pooled /
−26.36 % median vs −25.00 % / −24.31 % for the other 40. The ≈3-point separation sits far inside the
slice's own −12 % to −37 % spread. Reported inconclusive, deliberately not stretched. Sizing it needs
a purpose-built small-cold-cell sample, not a size-stratified one.

🔵 **Disk cost of untrimmed, measured for the first time:** `.sql` mean **20.0 MB**, median 8.1,
max 124.9 (the 90-zone Courthouse), ≈**1.40 MB/zone** → full fleet at 8,160 ≈ **159 GB**, far under
the >800 GB the register records for untrimmed `fast_zone` city passes. **Affordable. Whether to run
one is the USER'S call and was not taken.**

## 2026-08-19 (night, last) — CP-1 of `PLAN_gap-decomposition-2026-08-19.md`, and a CORRECTION to the section above

🔴 **The section immediately above overstates the cross-mode gap. Its figures are superseded.**
Read this before quoting −26.25 % or −24.40 % anywhere.

### The defect that caused it

`total_eui_kwh_m2` **undercounts Interior Lighting and Interior Equipment whenever a zone multiplier
exceeds 1.** Both are summed from per-zone hourly variables, which EnergyPlus does not
multiplier-scale, while the floor area they are divided by **is** multiplier-aware
(`openubem/results/parser.py:431-433`, `resolve_simulated_floor_area()`). Every other end use matches
the multiplier-correct ABUPS table to <0.01 %. **42 of 48 affected; 6 of 48 reconcile within 2 %;
error median 4.71 %, mean 17.56 %, max 192.28 %, 14 buildings over 10 %.**

⚠️ **The gate that catches this already exists and the fleet pipeline never calls it:**
`check_building_integrity()` (`openubem/results/parser.py:602-646`) returns `abups_ok=False` for the
worst offender. Several `scripts/run_*.py` call it; the path that produced these tables does not.

✅ **The adopted figure is safe, verified not assumed.** Zone multipliers are written in exactly one
module — `openubem/geometry/layout_assigner.py`, `match_storeys()`, `z_obj.Multiplier` at `:649`.
The director inspected three run-4 `auto` fleet IDFs (`…/open48_refleet4/nyc_rural/fleet_staging/idfs/`):
**every `Multiplier` field is 1.** **`153.8231 kWh/m²` over 8,153 is untouched.** Corollary:
**every `layout_assign` EUI this project has ever recorded is undercounted** — `layout_assign`-only,
a mode not on the production path.

### Corrected sample figures (n=48, multiplier-correct via ABUPS)

| cut | was | **corrected** |
|---|---|---|
| all 48 pooled | −26.25 % | **−11.26 %** |
| all 48 median | −24.40 % | **−17.72 %** |
| area agrees, n=21, pooled / median | −19.73 / −25.87 % | **−17.39 / −22.60 %** |
| area agrees excl. Courthouse, n=20 | −28.02 / −26.05 % | **−23.93 / −23.19 %** |
| NYC small, n=8, pooled / median | −27.98 / −26.36 % | **−6.68 / −18.48 %** |
| above `auto` | 1 of 48 | **8 of 48** |

**Survives:** the confounder conclusion — the denominator mismatch inflates the gap but is not its
cause; **≈−24 %** remains on the cleanest subset. **Withdrawn:** "about a quarter below" as a
sample-wide claim, and the claim that pooled and median agree within 2 points (they do not; the
median is more robust here).

### 🔴 Withdrawn: the executor's refutation of OPEN-03's attribution

T01 computed that vintage-correcting lighting+equipment moves total energy by at most **7.76 %**
(6.13 % offices) and called it well short of half the gap — **measured against the uncorrected
≈25 %.** Against the corrected median 17.72 %, half is 8.9 points and 7.76 is available.
**OPEN-03's "roughly half" is NOT contradicted overall; it still falls short against the −23.9 %
clean cut. Verdict: inconclusive, not refuted.** ⚠️ **This is the second time in two passes that an
executor's headline conclusion did not survive the director re-deriving it. Re-derive anything
load-bearing; do not adopt an executor's conclusion as published.**

### T02 — OPEN-59 answered, cleanly

All 290 `OpenUBEMUnknown` buildings in `nyc_suburban`, all four PDE columns (lighting, equipment,
occupancy, wwr): **0 of 4 columns show any out-of-bounds draw**, bit-identical to the prior
acceptance test where they overlap. **Hypothesis 1 (unscreened sibling columns) is refuted — the
donor screen applies uniformly at HEAD.** Favours hypothesis 2: the post-screen donor pool is
genuinely higher-intensity — **a calibration question, not a bounds defect.** This closes out the
diagnostic owed since the morning. **Recommending nothing; OPEN-59's disposition is the user's.**

### End-use shape (n=48, ABUPS, unaffected by the correction)

Interior Equipment 32.45 %, Fans 21.17 %, Cooling 15.63 %, Interior Lighting 13.86 %, Heating 9.09 %,
Water Systems 7.08 %, Pumps 0.73 %. Lighting+Equipment **46.31 %**; MidriseApartment (n=9) 41.21 %
vs offices (n=31) 57.30 %, MidriseApartment dominated by hot water 37.93 %.

### 🔴 Decisions owed to the user — ask ONE at a time, do NOT take them

1. **The zone-multiplier undercount in `total_eui_kwh_m2`** — tracked ID or not? It is a real code
   defect with a gate that already exists and is simply not wired into the fleet path.
2. **The ≈−24 % cross-mode gap on the clean subset** — tracked ID or not? Still unexplained.
3. **A full-fleet untrimmed `layout_assign` pass** (≈159 GB, costed at CP-1).
4. **OPEN-59's disposition** now that the bounds screen came back clean — calibration question.
5. Carried forward: the **OPEN-35 regression** ID and remedy; the **4 `Courthouse` → `OpenUBEMUnknown`
   flips** (477,804 m²); **OPEN-56's** remedy shape.

**Register state: 19 live / 40 struck / 59 total, next free `OPEN-60`, invariant *struck − retired = 2*
holds. Adopted fleet EUI: 153.8231 kWh/m² pooled over 8,153 buildings / 24,320,582 m² — NOT
volume-correct (OPEN-56's 10 m³ stub, ≈ +1.0 kWh/m²/building, sits outside it). Zero fitted parameters.**

**OPEN-60 opened** — `total_eui_kwh_m2` undercounts Interior Lighting and Interior Equipment wherever a
zone multiplier > 1. Per-zone hourly variables are not multiplier-scaled by EnergyPlus; the floor-area
divisor is (`openubem/results/parser.py:431-433`). 6 of 48 reconcile within 2 %; max error 192.28 %.
🟢 **Zero adopted rows affected — verified at IDF level**, not assumed: multipliers are written only in
`openubem/geometry/layout_assigner.py:649`, which `auto` never calls, and three run-4 `auto` fleet IDFs
carry `Multiplier = 1` throughout. **153.8231 kWh/m² pooled over 8,153 buildings stands.**

calls it** — that finding is not about multipliers and is the more general one.

buildings out of bounds, worst excursion 0.0; `openubem/outputs/comparisons/open59_pde_bounds_screen.csv`).
Not OPEN-55's sibling; every draw is legal so no screen can fix it. Hypothesis 2 stands by elimination —
**calibration question, not defect.** ID kept open because the screen answered *why not*, not *why*.

### The board had a rendering defect and it is fixed

🔴 **Fifteen rows carried a status with an appended correction note** (`"done <b>STATUS CORRECTED…"`).
`S[st][0]` matched nothing, threw, and **discarded the entire task list** while the counters above it
still rendered. **The user was reading counters with no tasks under them.** The renderer now takes the
state from the first token (`base(st)`) and renders the remainder as a chip. **If you ever add a status
annotation, this is why it works — do not revert it.**

### Board audit result

Of 24 unfinished rows, **19 were finished and merely unmarked**; each was checked against the register or
the source, not against its own note. **Left: 3 ready (C04 26 untested columns · C05 storey-cost, OPEN-35
· W9 evidence retention, now overdue because OPEN-53 shows 874/875 directories already lost their
evidence), 1 with the user (S03, who writes the rebuilt fixture's labels), 1 running (AA5).**
New board group **AA**, rows AA1–AA5. Artifact republished, label `aa-status-audit-and-render-fix`.

### What is running

`implemenation/PLAN_vintage-elasticity-2026-08-19.md` — **T01 dispatched to a Sonnet executor, stop at
CP-1.** Tests OPEN-03's central claim, which has never been tested in the life of the arc.

`openubem/data/loads/doe_prototype_loads.json` and `openstudio_loads.json` carry one load pair per
archetype and **no vintage key**. So the plan measures **elasticity** and **inverts** it — what would
loads have to be for the vintage story to explain half the gap. That framing is deliberate; do not
"improve" it into a vintage patch, which would mean inventing a table.

   headline did not survive it; the second time the re-derivation opened a new defect (OPEN-60) rather

## HANDOVER — 2026-08-20 (supersedes every earlier handover section in this file)

**Register state, re-derived programmatically after the last edit:** 20 live / 40 struck /
OPEN-01..OPEN-60 / **next free OPEN-61**. Invariant holds (struck rows − retired IDs = 2).

### What changed since the previous handover

1. **OPEN-03's central claim is REFUTED** (CP-2 ruling, `implemenation/PLAN_vintage-elasticity-2026-08-19.md`;
   full working in `extra/MEASUREMENT_open-03_load-elasticity.md` §CP-2). "Roughly half the cross-mode
   gap is vintage-blind internal loads" measures at **≈8 % pooled**. The decisive evidence is a control
   the experiment produced accidentally: **4 of 20 buildings have bit-identical internal loads across
   both modes and still show a −23.61 % gap.** The premise is wrong too — **both** modes are
   vintage-blind, they simply read loads from different sources that disagree ~2.6× on lighting.
   🔴 **Do not commission an era-dependent loads table on this item's account.** OPEN-03 **stays live**
   with its scope replaced: ~92 % of the gap is unexplained and known not to be loads.
2. **Board row C04 closed** — the 26 previously untestable result columns are compared
   (`extra/MEASUREMENT_open-06_twentysix-simulation-columns.md`). Result is a **bimodal fleet**, not a
   column pass/fail: 48.2 % of buildings unchanged within 0.1 %, 9.0 % moved >5 %.
3. **Board rows AA5/AA6/AA7 added or rewritten; C04 marked done.** Board validated (26 groups, 174 rows,
   0 unmappable statuses), both copies synced, artifact republished.

### Two standing lessons, both earned this pass

- 🔴 **A control on one exemplar cannot establish that an intervention reached a population.** The CP-1
  gate I wrote tested one building; it happened to be one of the 4 reachable ones, so it passed while
  the intervention was inert in 16 of 20. **Gate perturbation experiments on the count of responding
  units.**
- 🔴 **Verify the baseline fixture before dispatching a comparison — column count *and* last-touching
  commit.** A dispatch naming `step1/overAll/results/cases/` (20 columns) instead of
  `validations/overAll/results/phaseE/` (32 columns) produced a confident "NOT COMPARABLE" plus a
  phantom missing-fixture defect. **Both were false and both were the director's path error.** Commit
  `0df422e` **is** the commit that last touched the phaseE fixture; OPEN-06b's citation is sound —
  **do not reopen it.**
- The older lesson still holds and paid again twice this pass: **re-derive any executor conclusion that
  is load-bearing.** "25 of 26 columns differ" (measured at 1e-9, i.e. rounding) and "still
  inconclusive" (which the data refutes outright) both failed re-derivation.
